Check 4 greps `e2e/at-matrix/runs/`, which has never existed — run records live inside each `{Component}.md` below an append-only marker. Both evaluated by intent; both fail on intent too. **Third consecutive task with a broken done-check (R2-O3 check 4, R2-O4 check 2/D108), and the first where the break fails UNSAFE.** **The headline: a failed screen-reader run can no longer be published as a pass.** The `at-manual` resolver filtered `result !== 'unrun'` and **never read the value**, so a component whose every pairing a human recorded `fail` published `state: 'pass'` — measured by N1-O4 §6.2, latent only because 0 of 534 cells had ever run, and therefore guaranteed to corrupt the *first* honest AT session in the repository's history. `CellState` gains **`fail`** (5 → 6 values; closes N1-O4 #4 and N2-D2's D2-D4) and resolution moves out of an 886-line generator into **`resolveAtManual()`**, a pure function, because the defect survived a year precisely by being unreachable from a unit test. **Rule: never resolve upward** — any `fail`/`partial` → `fail`; any `blocked` → `present`; all-pass-but-incomplete → `present`; `pass` requires every task on every required pairing. **Two judgement calls made and recorded**: `partial` resolves to `fail` (pass requires every step passed; the nuance survives in the tester's row), and **`fail` outranks `stale`** (a failure not re-run against newer code is still the last thing anybody observed — demoting it to the neutral-reading `stale` launders it exactly as `pass` did). **Seeded proof, old logic vs new on identical inputs**: all-`fail` **pass → fail** · all-`partial` **pass → fail** · all-`blocked` **pass → present** · 1-of-6 at Tier C **pass → present** · all-`unrun` unrun → unrun. Four of five were wrong before; all five now assert in a 22-spec regression file. **Tier-differentiated pairings landed in *contracts*, not the scaffold** (`TIER_AT_PAIR_INCREMENT` + `requiredAtPairs()`, public API addition), accumulating from A upward so **D ⊇ C ⊇ B is a property of the data structure**: B `nvda-firefox` · C + `jaws-chrome`, `voiceover-safari` · D all six. Closes N1-O4 #6, where the sole Tier D component (`DzFileUpload`, a data boundary) owed exactly what a Tier B badge owed and the matrix stamped `origin: "tier B"` to say so. **The denominator was NOT shrunk**: all 89 components keep a row for all six pairings — **534 cells, unchanged** — and `validate:at-matrix` now prints **both** numbers permanently (`cells 534 · required cells 136`), because narrowing to 136 would move a ratchet by redefinition rather than by work. **Run records gained a `task` column** (7 → 8; closes N1-O4 finding A1/#9 — the header had instructed "one row per {task, pair}" since day one with no column to put one in). **`migrateResultsTable()` widens a table only when every row is `unrun`** — one recorded run anywhere and the table is untouched — and `parseResults` accepts both widths for ever, since a skipped 7-column row would read as "never executed", the same falsehood from the other direction. **`DzSidebar` mis-declared `treeview` and never implemented one** (ships `role="navigation"`; APG recommends explicitly against the tree pattern for page links) → **`landmarks`**, which gained `ACTIVATE` so the `aria-current` contract stays testable; its `select` step became `activate` and `typeahead` folded into a widened `navigate` — **all seven expectations preserved**, and N1-O4's capitalised "do not re-file this mismatch" warning deleted as obsolete (closes #7). **`DzCommandPalette`'s `error` task** — the only not-applicable step in all 126, previously prose telling the tester to write a note no gate could count — is now a machine-readable waiver in `AT_TASK_OPT_OUTS` **printed in the scaffold header**, with a 3.3.1 override so it cannot silence a real obligation (closes #8). **`validate:at-scripts` chained into `validate:all`** as link 18, before `at-matrix` (42 → 43 links; closes #10). **N2-D2's tripwire was re-aimed, not deleted — and had to be**: its first refusal shape ("cell ≠ `unrun` while a row is `fail`") would have turned `validate:docs-pages` **red on the first honest failure** now that `fail` is the correct state; it now refuses only a cell reading *better* than its rows, and is kept as the only cross-artifact check between the matrix and the scaffold. **`eslint e2e/` 142 → 9**, and it was never lint noise: all 133 were `no-multiple-empty-lines` from **two off-by-one newlines in the two generators** (`${renderKeyboardCitation(row)}\n## Pairs`; `${steps}`/`${defects}` double-terminated). Fixed at source — **no ignore rule added, no rule disabled** — and the surviving 9 are exactly the README's original `.ts` baseline. **Ratchets**: `CellState` 5 → **6** · AT index schema 1.0.0 → **1.1.0** · run-record columns 7 → **8** · tiers differentiating AT pairings 0/4 → **4/4** · `validate:all` links 42 → **43** · components with a wrong APG declaration 1 → **0** · machine-readable task waivers 0 → **1** · AT scripts 22/22 held, steps 126 → **124** (−2, both obligations that were never real) · `eslint e2e/` 142 → **9** · `yarn test` 10 146 → **10 164** · changesets 33 → **34** · **AT cells executed 0/534 → 0/534, required 0/136 — deliberately unmoved; only a human moves it** · `at-manual` cells **89 `unrun`, zero fabricated** · capability cells held at 1 662 · ownership **1 338 @ `2d51eec`**, untouched. **Aggregate**: `validate:all` **exit 1 at the same link as at `2d51eec`** (`capability-matrix`, 1 violation — `DzFileUpload` Tier-D, R2-O1's); **all 24 links after it individually exit 0**; `yarn test` **10 161/10 164, 3 failures ALL inherited by name** (`dzup-resolution`, `landing-token-fallbacks`, `story-dod-tiers countOpen`); `typecheck` **0**, `lint` **0**; `packages/tooling` tsc **12, all pre-existing** (3 introduced, 3 fixed, 0 net). **`storybook:test`/`test:e2e`/visual/perf deliberately not run** — the diff touches tooling, one contracts module, generated docs and the AT scaffold; no component's rendered output changes, so re-observing 1 451/1 453 would evidence nothing. **NO AT RESULT CELL WAS WRITTEN — 0 of 534, recorded as a refusal per `<no_fill>`.** 2.5.7 and security-corpus lanes deliberately **not** migrated onto the new `fail` state (D2-D4 named three lanes; only AT is in scope) → routed to R2-O5 and R3-O4. Pro untouched. Decisions **D109–D116**; **D112 (name a tester) is the gate and the only thing standing between this repo and the first non-zero AT number it has ever had.** Nothing committed. |

Check 2's glob could never have passed — composables live one directory deep.** **9 of 11 N1-O1 defects + E6 + all 3 storybook failures closed; D4 and D10 recorded, gated and routed (D98/D99).** **D8 fixed at the source**: `useDualModel` wrote to BOTH models, so a `v-model:value`-only consumer latched the first user edit into the unbound default model and every later external write was discarded — a silent data-loss bug in **seven** public controls. The fix is a last-written sentinel, four lines, no watcher and no signature change: *a model that has moved away from what I wrote was moved by the parent, and the parent wins* — symmetric, so it is correct for legacy-only / default-only / both / neither without branching. Both refs are read unconditionally in the getter so the computed tracks both. **R5-O6's pin discharged as designed**: it was written to go red on the fix, it did, the expected-failure test was **deleted not worked around**, and `it.skip('the contract, once D8 is fixed')` is now live. `checkExternalWrite` was **not** weakened — its refusal of a snapshot-only trace stands, and every new spec is a trace. `D8_CONTROLS` was not dropped but **inverted** into `DUAL_MODEL_CONTROLS`, which now fails if a `useDualModel` consumer has no D8 spec of its own — an eighth control cannot join silently. **`yarn typecheck` found a defect the register never named**: making `DzResizableContext.disabled` **required** broke `DzSplitter`, which provides the same context and had D2 identically. **D11's real shape**: `:id="id"` handed an explicit `undefined` down to the Reka component and a fallthrough attr beats the component's own binding, so Reka's generated content id was **erased** — measured: the menu carried no `id` at all. Fixed in 5 overlay content components by folding the id into the existing attrs spread (a second `v-bind` is a duplicate-attribute compile error). **A separate upstream defect measured and deliberately NOT worked around**: `reka-ui@2.9.2` assigns `rootContext.contentId` on a non-reactive context in setup, so a `defaultOpen` menu ships an empty `aria-controls` forever; 65 sub-part rows refused with a reason — a row owes its tier's whole evidence set and `DzMenuItem` has no story by design, so it would arrive owing ~400 cells that are unwritable rather than unwritten (D107 asks for the gate). **CSP lane built, and it found something** (O5-5 closed): one Vite build served twice from one Node server, `/open/` bare and `/strict/` under a real header with a per-request nonce and no `'unsafe-inline'`; `DzThemeProvider` + `DzFileUpload` render identically across 18 computed properties, no un-nonced `<style>` survives, and the URL policy holds in chromium/firefox/webkit. **The expectation that failed is the finding**: `DzButton`'s `style="contain: layout style"` is NOT dropped, because Vue writes a static template style through CSSOM and **CSP does not govern CSSOM** — while a control element whose attribute is in the served markup IS dropped, which proves the directive is enforced. **So F-C1's 78+38 are an SSR exposure, not 116 client-side failures**: the same components emit a literal `style=` in server-rendered markup, the parser applies it, and a strict policy removes the containment on the first paint before hydration (**D105**). **F-C1 measured and dispositioned**: `inline-style-inventory.json` — 78 `recipe-movable` · 3 `layout-static` · 19 `required-dynamic` · 33 `unclassified-binding` (named rather than folded into `required-dynamic`, which would claim more than was measured) · 0 `custom-property`; ## Owner decisions raised by this program

> **Appended 2026-09-22 (TASK-R0-O1).** This table runs to **D153**. The six R1
> packets of 2026-09-21 raised **D154–D178**, and TASK-R0-O1 raised **D179–D186**;
> all are consolidated with the N1/N2/N5 backlog, grouped by what they gate, in
> [`./reports/owner-decision-register-2026-09.md`](./reports/owner-decision-register-2026-09.md)
> — **⛔ said "298 decisions, 217 open"; it is 314 raised / 233 open** since the
> 2026-09-22 amendment appended `D179`–`D192` (which the register, written by
> TASK-R0-O1, omitted — including R0-O1's own eight; finding **S3**) and raised
> `D193`–`D194`. The register cites this table by path and does not restate it.
>
> **Appended 2026-09-22 (TASK-R0-O2), the programme's last task.** Six more,
> **D187–D192**, continuing from D186 and set out in full in
> [`./reports/TASK-R0-O2-handoff.md`](./reports/TASK-R0-O2-handoff.md) §7:
> **D187** 🟠 do the two tiers share one ADR number space or split it (ADR-13 is
> resolved by naming; the next collision is not) — *rec. reserve Pro a band* ·
> **D188** 🔴 are the three ADR acceptances one decision or three — *rec. sign
> ADR-19 and ADR-20 now (no unmet precondition but the signature), hold ADR-18
> behind the Node floor*; this is the one that moves **C1 0/3 → 2/3** ·
> **D189** 🟢 should `adr-registry.json` mirror each ADR's status — *rec. no, as
> built: the document's `Status:` line is the one source* · **D190** 🟠 66
> components now let `aria-invalid` fall through to `$attrs` with no dev-mode
> warning — *rec. warn on the ~12 status-role components* · **D191** 🟢 is
> `maxProposedCitedFromCode` the right meter (distinct ADRs, not citation
> sites) — *rec. as built* · **D192** 🟠 confirm or reverse **D10**, taken as
> option (A) under delegation — *rec. confirm*.
>
> With these the register stands at **314 raised / 233 open**. **The register
> did not carry their rows, and it did not carry TASK-R0-O1's own eight
> either** — `grep -c` for `D179`…`D192` in it returned **0** on 2026-09-22
> while its front matter declared itself complete (finding **S3**). All
> fourteen were appended that day into §2.5, §3.5 and §5.6, grouped by what
> they gate. **D183** (is the register the system of record?) is the decision
> that would stop the drift recurring, and it is still open.
>
> **Appended 2026-09-22 (release-exit fix pass).** Two more, **D193**–**D194**,
> continuing from D192 and set out in the register §5.7. Both are the same
> shape — **a gate clause that is printed like a check and cannot fail**:
> **D193** 🟠 the icon gate's `[lock-vs-disk]` cross-check is `report`-level, so
> a duplication present in `node_modules` but hidden by the lockfile passes
> (proved by seeding) — *rec. leave it `report` and stop the module header
> calling it "a second, independent route"; promoting it is the owner's call
> because it would add a second error clause to the one gate R1-O6 leaves
> deliberately red* · **D194** 🟠 `validate:registry`'s 9th clause (`docs`) is
> report-only, has no seeded case, and is firing on **103 of 191** items while
> the gate prints `✓` and exits 0 — *rec. ratchet it at 103 and fail on a rise,
> the `anatomy-parts` shape D143 asks for; promoting it outright lands 103 reds
> and is the `llms.txt` surface's question (N2-A3 D2), not this gate's*.
>
> **Two warnings for anyone reading ids here.** (1) **`D43` and `D44` were never
> issued** — no reference exists in any of the 83 programme files; the gap is
> deliberate and should stay. (2) **`D<n>` is not a unique key.** The defect
> register (`D1`–`D11`) and this decision sequence collide: `D8`, `D9`, `D10`
> and `D11` each name a *defect* in `TASK-R2-O3-handoff.md` **and** a *decision*
> here or in `TASK-R5-O1-handoff.md`. Rows `D98` and `D99` below open with
> `**D4 --` and `**D10 --`, which are the **defects**, not these rows. See

This is the enforcement half of **D127**, recorded as a defect because the ledger currently reads as protection that does not exist. **Adjacent weakness in the same function**: the bare `catch {}` at `packages/tooling/src/validators/capability-matrix.ts:103-105` swallows a **corrupt or unparseable** HEAD ledger identically to a missing one — a truncated or hand-edited committed ledger silently turns the gate back off, with the same reassuring "first landing" wording | independent verification 2026-09-19 (TASK-R2-O1) | (a) **rec.** — commit the ledger (owner, D127), then **re-run `yarn validate:capability-matrix` and confirm the note reads *"N committed `pass` cell(s) compared"* and not *"no committed browser ledger"*; that sentence is the only evidence the gate is live** · (b) additionally, **distinguish the two `catch` cases**: `git show` exiting non-zero because the path is absent is a legitimate first landing; a `JSON.parse` failure on bytes git *did* return is a corrupt committed ledger and must **fail**, not report inert · (c) assert the live wording in `capability-matrix.spec.ts` so the gate cannot silently return to inert | open |
| D142 🟠 | **`DzTableCell` puts `role="separator"` on a `<button>`, and nothing in this repository would catch it.** `packages/core/src/components/data/DzTableCell.vue:278-292` renders `<button type="button" … role="separator" aria-orientation aria-valuenow aria-valuemin>` (the role is on `:285`). **ARIA in HTML does not permit `separator` on `<button>`** — the allowed roles are `button`, `checkbox`, `combobox`, `link`, `menuitem`, `menuitemcheckbox`, `menuitemradio`, `option`, `radio`, `switch`, `tab` — so axe's `aria-allowed-role` could flag it. That rule is **not in `BLOCK_GATED_RULES`** (`apps/landing/src/lib/axeGates.ts:102-110`), and **no landing block renders a resizable table column** — the one `resizable` block, `blocks/layout/ResizableWorkspace.vue`, uses `DzResizable`/`DzResizableHandle`, which sit on Reka's `SplitterResizeHandle` (a `<div>`, where the role is permitted) — so the markup is never rendered into the axe lane at all. **This is not a mistake by TASK-R2-O5**: it satisfies that task's requirement exactly as written, and the sibling `DzResizableHandle` has no equivalent problem. The cost is an unmeasured conformance risk on **the very component whose SC 2.5.7 evidence now depends on this markup**, and there is precedent — `axeGates.ts:126` already records an open, ungated `aria-allowed-role` instance (`DzMention` puts `role="combobox"` on a `<textarea>`), logged as a core-library fix | independent verification 2026-09-19 (TASK-R2-O5) | (a) keep the `<button>` and **gate the rule** — add `aria-allowed-role` to `BLOCK_GATED_RULES` and land a block that renders a resizable column, which also closes the pre-existing `DzMention` instance · (b) move the affordance to an element that permits the role (a `<div role="separator" tabindex="0">` with the keyboard handlers kept) and re-run the non-drag lane · (c) accept and record the deviation in `wcag-deviations.json`. **rec. (a)** — it measures the question instead of answering it, and gets `DzMention` for free. **Do not change the markup silently**: the SC 2.5.7 record (`wcag-deviations.json`, `e2e/matrix/non-drag.spec.ts`, TASK-R2-O5 §9.2 step 3) cites this exact element, so a change must move the evidence with it | open |

## Independent verification, 2026-09-19

Full report: [`./reports/TASK-R2-verification-2026-09-19.md`](./reports/TASK-R2-verification-2026-09-19.md).
Filed as a report rather than a ledger section because it crosses seven tasks and
belongs to none of them; README §5 `<handoff>` keeps this file as the index and
puts the evidence under `reports/`.

**22 claims measured at `2d51eec` + dirty tree. 1 refuted, 15 discrepancies
corrected, 6 defects registered, 0 behaviour changed.**

- **Refuted: claim 12, as worded.** *"raw colour literals 5 → 0"* reads as
  removal. **Nothing was removed.** All five literals are still in
  `packages/tooling/src/perf/tier-fixtures.ts:152,154` and are meant to be — they
  are `DzColorPicker`'s `modelValue`/`presets`, values the component *parses*,
  in a fixture that exists to isolate its paint cost, so a `var(--dz-*)` string
  would mount an empty shell and measure nothing. What moved 5 → 0 is the
  **violation count**, via the pre-existing line-scoped
  `// token-check-disable-line` directive. Reframed everywhere it appeared.
- **11 discrepancies were reported; 15 were measured here**, three of the
  reported items carrying more than one wrong number and one defect (D139) being
  found while checking claim 12. Ranked in the report §2. The top five:
  **(1)** claim 12's framing · **(2)** *"the other 21 fixtures stay armed"* —
  false, the rejection comment applies the file-scoped marker (**D139**) ·
  **(3)** `perf-bench.spec.ts:117`'s *"`size` … gates always"* — false, nothing
  asserts a `size:*` metric anywhere (**D140**, corrected in place) ·
  **(4)** `browser-degradation` is proven code with **zero protection** while its
  baseline is untracked (**D141**) · **(5)** `known-failures.json`'s *"28 entries
  stand"* — that file is `entries: []`; the 28 **stay closed**.
- **Corrected numbers**, each re-measured before being written: unit tests
  **77 → 102 (+25)**, not +22 (`DzResizable` 9 → 21 · `DzSplitter` 13 → 19 ·
  `DzTable` 55 → 62; confirmed by `yarn vitest run` → 102 passed) · i18n catalog
  **110 → 116** against HEAD, not 112 → 116 · `locales/` holds **`de.json` and
  `en.json` only** — no `pseudo.json` · `validate:all` has **44 links**, not 37 ·
  `dirtyPathCount` 427 vs 443 at one `sourceCommit` are **both honest snapshots**
  and were **not** rewritten.
- **Defects D139–D144 registered above.** None implemented — each is an owner
  call or a follow-on packet, and four would change gate behaviour.
- **Structural invariants, each verified rather than assumed:** nothing
  committed (HEAD still `2d51eec`), pushed or published · no baseline accepted
  and no `visual:accept` · no workflow edited (`git status -- .github/` empty) ·
  **AT cells 0 of 534**, `required executed 0` · no ceiling raised · no exception
  added, and **no new `eslint-disable`, `token-check-*` marker or `test.fail()`
  written anywhere** · no suppression silencing a real failure — the only code
  edit is a doc-comment that *removes* a false claim · all pre-existing dirty
  work preserved (**448 → 451 paths**; the three added are `README.md` and
  `perf-bench.spec.ts`, tracked and clean before this pass edited them, and the
  verification report itself).
- **`yarn validate:all` — exit 0, 44 of 44 links green**, run end to end with the
  exit code read from a file.

### Sweep 2026-09-18 (b) — the three `[!]` rows, investigated and worked

Each blocked row was investigated separately to answer "is this genuinely
blocked, or only recorded as blocked?" **One was mis-recorded, one was
understated, one was exactly right.**

| Task | Investigated verdict | Work done this session |
|---|---|---|
| **R3-O1** | **Understated.** Pro TASK-R1-P4 is not "one generator run" — Pro has **no ownership generator at all**, so it is a ~300-400 line packet (its inputs are already enumerable: 1,012 distinct symbols in Pro's `public-api.manifest.json`). C7 stays blocked. **But the OSS merge path carried two untested defects.** | **F3** — `buildRuntimeLookup` discarded `map.collisions`, so an unresolved cross-tier collision silently dropped a symbol from the resolver while the generator exited 0 and `validate:ownership` stayed green; now surfaced, and the CLI + validator fail on it. **F2** — the same path never passed `readCollisionDecisions()`, so a recorded decision was honoured by `generate:ownership:map` and ignored by `generate:ownership`. **25 new specs** (21 + 4) on two functions that had **zero**. Seeded-and-proved both. Plus **D7**: the collision list is **not** empty — `CalendarView` overlaps Core and Pro's `public-api.manifest.json`, the file P4 derives from; the "zero overlapping symbols, verified 2026-08-20" claim was stale in **three** records, all corrected. |
| **R5-O4** | **Exactly right — nothing executable remains.** `de.json` is a deliberate scaffold (0/110 translated, 110 explicit fallbacks) and is structurally unpublishable: rule 9 rejects a scaffold named in `exports` and the build emits only `en.json`. Filling it is the one act an agent is *forbidden* ("no machine translation", `CONTRIBUTING.md:101`). | None. D59 needs a named human translator; D63 is an owner A/B/C pick. |
| **R5-O9** | **Right that D91 is unavoidable** — no in-scope route clears the four bars: writing the tests means ~1,350 branches, ~48 % of the landing half in code `vitest.config.ts:134-144` documents as jsdom-**unreachable**; the coverage-v8 switch is gone in v4 and its peers are exact-pinned. **But D91 was framed backwards.** | **Evidence preserved** — the only per-file record of the phase-2 measurement lived in a session scratchpad due to be reaped; both readings are now committed under `reports/TASK-R5-O9-baseline/` and **re-aggregate to §7.4's figures exactly**. **D91's second reading executed** (D97 rec (a)): `vitest@3.2.6` + `--coverage.experimentalAstAwareRemapping=true`, **no dependency changed**, reproduces the Vitest-4 basis — six of ten packages to the digit, aggregate 76.66 % vs 76.62 % — and **exits 1**, i.e. the thresholds fail on the *committed* runner. Handoff §7.4b/§7.4c. |

**The finding that should reach the owner before D91 is signed:** the coverage
drop is **not a regression and not Vitest 4's doing**. The denominators moved
(statements 90,746 → 34,328; functions 4,103 → 11,014) and under 3.2.6
`statements === lines` *exactly* — the signature of v8 counting compiled output.
Vitest 4 makes AST-aware remapping unconditional. No test regressed; branch
coverage in `packages/*/src/**` was **always ≈76.6 %**, and the 84.66 % that
justified the 80 bar was compiled-output inflation. D91 is therefore
*"re-baseline onto a truthful basis"*, not *"lower a bar to make a build pass"* —
which is the thing `vitest.config.ts:145-146` forbids. It also means **the
re-baseline and the runner upgrade are independent decisions**: the four numbers
can be moved on 3.2.6 today, after which Vitest 4 is coverage-neutral.

**Also fixed (tooling lane, found via R5-O4's `<validation>` block):**
`validate:tree-shake` used `bundleContent.includes(sentinel)`, so the i18n
catalog's message-group keys `DzDataGridHeader` / `DzDataGridPagination`
(`packages/core/src/i18n/messages.ts`) made every single-component bundle look
as if `DzDataGrid` had leaked. The gate failed on correctly tree-shaken bundles
and **could not tell that case from a real leak**. Replaced with an
identifier-aware matcher extracted to `packages/tooling/src/tree-shake-sentinel.ts`
(its own module because `tree-shake-check.ts` self-executes on import), **+12
specs**. `validate:tree-shake` now **exit 0** — 4/4 bundles PASS — where it had
been red since P4-03. Tracked `.tree-shake-test/` scaffold verified restored.
